\displaystyle=\!\int_{\mathsf{x}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}\\
\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\end{array}\right)^{T}\!\!M\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}\\
\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\end{array}\right)=\!\int_{x}\Big\{\lambda_{+}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|^{2}+\lambda_{-}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{2}\Big\},

where

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(r_{\mathrm{ip}}+r_{\mathrm{op}}\pm\sqrt{\left(r_{\mathrm{ip}}-r_{\mathrm{op}}\right)^{2}+r_{\mathrm{io}1}^{2}+r_{\mathrm{io}2}^{2}}\,\right).

(343)

Here, we used the result
U^{T}MU=\mathrm{diag}\left(\lambda_{+},\lambda_{+},\lambda_{-},\lambda_{-}\right)

and introduced the diagonal basis

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\\
\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=U^{T}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}\\
\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\beta_{+}R_{\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}+\beta_{-}\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\\
-\beta_{-}R_{\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}+\beta_{+}\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\end{array}\right),

with the rotation matrix

\displaystyle R_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\cos\gamma&-\sin\gamma\\
\sin\gamma&\cos\gamma\end{smallmatrix}\right),

(350)

and the unitary matrix

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\beta_{+}\cos\delta_{\mathrm{io}}&-\beta_{+}\sin\delta_{\mathrm{io}}&-\beta_{-}\cos\delta_{\mathrm{io}}&\beta_{-}\sin\delta_{\mathrm{io}}\\
\beta_{+}\sin\delta_{\mathrm{io}}&\beta_{+}\cos\delta_{\mathrm{io}}&-\beta_{-}\sin\delta_{\mathrm{io}}&-\beta_{-}\cos\delta_{\mathrm{io}}\\
\beta_{-}&0&\beta_{+}&0\\
0&\beta_{-}&0&\beta_{+}\end{smallmatrix}\right),

(351)

where

\displaystyle\beta_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{1\pm\frac{r_{\mathrm{ip}}-r_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sqrt{\left(r_{\mathrm{ip}}-r_{\mathrm{op}}\right)^{2}+r_{\mathrm{io}1}^{2}+r_{\mathrm{io}2}^{2}}}}\,,

(352)

and

\displaystyle\cos\delta_{\mathrm{io}}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathrm{io}1}}{\sqrt{r_{\mathrm{io}1}^{2}+r_{\mathrm{io}2}^{2}}},

\displaystyle\sin\delta_{\mathrm{io}}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{\mathrm{io}2}}{\sqrt{r_{\mathrm{io}1}^{2}+r_{\mathrm{io}2}^{2}}}.

(353)

We emphasize that the corresponding expressions shown in Sec.
IV.3

for the trigonal groups {
\mathsf{D_{3}}
,
\mathsf{D_{3d}}
,
\mathsf{C_{3v}}
}
can be recovered from the expressions above upon setting
r_{\mathrm{io}2}=0
,
which gives
\delta_{\mathrm{io}}=0,\>\pi
. The cubic and quartic
actions can also be rewritten in the
\boldsymbol{d}^{\pm}=\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\pm}\right|\left(\cos\gamma_{\pm},\sin\gamma_{\pm}\right)^{T}

basis. For instance,
\mathcal{S}_{3}
becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|^{3}\left[g_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}\mathsf{c}_{3\gamma_{+}}+g_{+}^{\mathrm{s}}\mathsf{s}_{3\gamma_{+}}\right]+\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{3}\left[g_{-}^{\mathrm{c}}\mathsf{c}_{3\gamma_{-}}+g_{-}^{\mathrm{s}}\mathsf{s}_{3\gamma_{-}}\right]

\displaystyle+\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|^{2}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|\left[\tilde{g}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}}\mathsf{c}_{2\gamma_{+}+\gamma_{-}}+\tilde{g}_{1}^{\mathrm{s}}\mathsf{s}_{2\gamma_{+}+\gamma_{-}}\right]

\displaystyle+\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{2}\left[\tilde{g}_{2}^{\mathrm{c}}\mathsf{c}_{\gamma_{+}+2\gamma_{-}}+\tilde{g}_{2}^{\mathrm{s}}\mathsf{s}_{\gamma_{+}+2\gamma_{-}}\right]\Big\}.

(354)

Here, we defined the new Landau coefficients

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}_{+}

\displaystyle=\beta_{-}^{3}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{op}}+\beta_{-}^{2}\beta_{+}R_{\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}+\beta_{-}\beta_{+}^{2}R_{2\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}+\beta_{+}^{3}R_{3\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{ip}},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}_{-}

\displaystyle=\beta_{+}^{3}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{op}}-\beta_{-}\beta_{+}^{2}R_{\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}+\beta_{-}^{2}\beta_{+}R_{2\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}-\beta_{-}^{3}R_{3\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{ip}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1}

\displaystyle=3\beta_{-}^{2}\beta_{+}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{op}}+\beta_{-}\left(2\beta_{+}^{2}-\beta_{-}^{2}\right)R_{\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}

\displaystyle+\beta_{+}\left(\beta_{+}^{2}-2\beta_{-}^{2}\right)R_{2\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}-3\beta_{-}\beta_{+}^{2}R_{3\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{ip}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{2}

\displaystyle=3\beta_{-}\beta_{+}^{2}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{op}}+\beta_{+}\left(\beta_{+}^{2}-2\beta_{-}^{2}\right)R_{\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{2}

\displaystyle-\beta_{-}\left(2\beta_{+}^{2}-\beta_{-}^{2}\right)R_{2\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{1}+3\beta_{-}^{2}\beta_{+}R_{3\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}^{T}\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{ip}},

(355)

where

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{ip}}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}g_{\mathrm{ip}}^{\mathrm{c}}\\
g_{\mathrm{ip}}^{\mathrm{s}}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}g_{\mathrm{op}}^{\mathrm{c}}\\
g_{\mathrm{op}}^{\mathrm{s}}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}g_{1,2}^{\mathrm{c}}\\
g_{1,2}^{\mathrm{s}}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}g_{\pm}^{\mathrm{c}}\\
g_{\pm}^{\mathrm{s}}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\tilde{g}_{1,2}^{\mathrm{c}}\\
\tilde{g}_{1,2}^{\mathrm{s}}\end{array}\right).

Similarly to the case of the trigonal groups investigated in Sec.
IV.3
,
we keep only the terms in the action that are linear and quadratic
in the sub-leading order parameter
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}
. This results
in
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}_{-}\left[\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right]+\mathcal{S}_{+-}\left[\boldsymbol{d}^{+},\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right]

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{-}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{\lambda_{-}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{2}+g_{-}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{3}\cos\left(3\gamma_{-}-\delta_{-}\right)+u_{-}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{4}\Big\},

(366)

where

\displaystyle g_{-}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sign}\left(g_{-}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\sqrt{\left(g_{-}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)^{2}+\left(g_{-}^{\mathrm{s}}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\delta_{-}

\displaystyle=\arctan\frac{g_{-}^{\mathrm{s}}}{g_{-}^{\mathrm{c}}},

(367)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{+-}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{\lambda_{+}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|^{2}+\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|^{2}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|\tilde{g}_{1}\cos\left(2\gamma_{+}+\gamma_{-}-\tilde{\delta}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{2}\tilde{g}_{2}\cos\left(\gamma_{+}+2\gamma_{-}-\tilde{\delta}_{2}\right)\Big\},

(368)

with relationships similar to Eq. (
367
) holding
for
\tilde{g}_{1,2}
and
\tilde{\delta}_{1,2}
in terms of the
vectors
\tilde{\boldsymbol{g}}_{1,2}
defined in Eq. (
355
).
Minimizing with respect to
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}
gives

\displaystyle\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|

\displaystyle\approx\frac{-\tilde{g}_{2}\cos\left(\gamma_{+}+2\gamma_{-}-\tilde{\delta}_{2}\right)}{2\lambda_{+}}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{3}\right),

(369)

which, when inserted back in Eq. (
368
), results
in an additional quartic term in
\boldsymbol{d}^{-}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{+-}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{-\frac{\left(\tilde{g}_{2}\right)^{2}\cos^{2}\left(\gamma_{+}+2\gamma_{-}-\tilde{\delta}_{2}\right)}{4\lambda_{+}}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|^{4}\Big\}.

(370)

Combined with the condition that
\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\right|

in Eq. (
370
) must be positive, this additional
quartic term is minimized for the angle

\displaystyle\gamma_{+}

\displaystyle=-2\gamma_{-}+\tilde{\delta}_{2}+\left(\frac{1+\mathrm{sign}\,\tilde{g}_{2}}{2}\right)\pi.

(371)

Consequently, the effect of integrating out the fluctuations in the
sub-leading
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}
channel is to renormalize the quartic
Landau coefficient
u_{-}\rightarrow u_{-}-\left(\tilde{g}_{2}\right)^{2}/(4\lambda_{+})

of the
\boldsymbol{d}^{-}
action (
366
), analogously
to what we found in Sec.
IV.3
. The main
difference of this action with respect to the action derived in Sec.
IV.3

for the trigonal groups {
\mathsf{D_{3}}
,
\mathsf{D_{3d}}
,
\mathsf{C_{3v}}
}
is the offset angle
\delta_{-}
. Thus, the nematic order parameter

\boldsymbol{d}^{-}
behaves as a
Z_{3}^{*}
-clock order parameter,
characterized by the threefold-degenerate ground state:

\displaystyle\gamma_{-}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{-}}{3}+\frac{\pi}{3}\frac{1+\mathrm{sign}\left(g_{-}\right)}{2}+\frac{2\pi}{3}n,

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle=\{0,1,2\}.

(372)

For these values of
\gamma_{-}^{0}
, the induced
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}

nematic order parameter becomes

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{d}^{+}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sign}\left(g_{-}\right)\frac{\left|\tilde{g}_{2}\right|}{2\lambda_{+}}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right|\mathcal{R}_{\tilde{\delta}_{2}-\delta_{-}}\boldsymbol{d}^{-}.

(373)

Therefore,
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}
is rotated against
\boldsymbol{d}^{-}

by the offset angle
\tilde{\delta}_{2}-\delta_{-}
. Using Eq. (
F
),
it is straightforward to obtain the original nematic order parameter
doublets:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\beta_{-}\boldsymbol{d}^{+}+\beta_{+}\boldsymbol{d}^{-},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}

\displaystyle=R_{-\delta_{\mathrm{io}}}\left(\beta_{+}\boldsymbol{d}^{+}-\beta_{-}\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\right).

(374)

Because
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}
is not collinear to
\boldsymbol{d}^{-}
,

\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}
and
\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}

are generally not going to be collinear either. One consequence of
this property is that the nematic axes
\boldsymbol{n}
,
\boldsymbol{m}
,
\boldsymbol{l}

will be offset from any high-symmetry axes. This is consistent with
the fact that the groups {
\mathsf{S_{6}}
,
\mathsf{C_{3}}
}
have no residual symmetry axes in the nematic phase, see also Table
1
.
In contrast, for the trigonal groups {
\mathsf{D_{3}}
,
\mathsf{D_{3d}}
,

\mathsf{C_{3v}}
}, the Landau coefficients satisfy
r_{\mathrm{io}2}=0
,

g_{\mathrm{ip},\mathrm{op},1,2}^{\mathrm{s}}=0
and
u_{\mathrm{io},1,2}^{\mathrm{s}}=0
.
This causes the rotation matrix in Eq. (
373
)
to become the identity,
\mathcal{R}_{\tilde{\delta}_{2}-\delta_{-}}=\mathbbm{1}
,
such that
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}
and
\boldsymbol{d}^{-}
are collinear.

Appendix G
Landau expansion for the tetragonal groups
\mathsf{C_{4h}}
,
\mathsf{C_{4}}

and
\mathsf{S_{4}}

In this Appendix, we consider the three tetragonal point groups {
\mathsf{C_{4h}}
,

\mathsf{C_{4}}
,
\mathsf{S_{4}}
} that do not possess in-plane
two-fold rotational symmetry axes. This implies not only that the
two in-plane nematic components
\left\{d_{2},d_{5}\right\}
transform
as the same one-dimensional IR, but also that the out-of-plane nematic
doublet
\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}
transforms as a complex IR.
For concreteness, we focus on the group
\mathsf{C_{4h}}
, for which

d_{2}
and
d_{5}
transform as
B_{g}
while
\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}=\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|e^{\mathsf{i}\gamma_{\mathrm{op}}}

and
\bar{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}=\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|e^{-\mathsf{i}\gamma_{\mathrm{op}}}

transform as
E_{g}
and
\bar{E}_{g}
.

Since the in-plane components are degenerate, we consider the full
in-plane doublet
\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}=\left(d_{2},d_{5}\right)
,
which transforms according to
(B_{g}\oplus B_{g})
. The decomposition
of the symmetrized product is straightforward:

\displaystyle\big[\otimes_{j=1}^{2}\left(B_{g}\oplus B_{g}\right)\big]_{s}

\displaystyle=3A_{g},

(375)

\displaystyle\big[\otimes_{j=1}^{3}\left(B_{g}\oplus B_{g}\right)\big]_{s}

\displaystyle=4B_{g},

(376)

\displaystyle\big[\otimes_{j=1}^{4}\left(B_{g}\oplus B_{g}\right)\big]_{s}

\displaystyle=5A_{g}.

(377)

There are thus
8
Landau invariants up to fourth-order. The resulting
action
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}_{2}+\mathcal{S}_{4}
is then:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{r_{1}\left(d_{2}\right)^{2}+r_{2}\left(d_{5}\right)^{2}+r_{3}d_{2}d_{5}\Big\},

(378)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{4}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{u_{1}\left(d_{2}\right)^{4}+u_{2}\left(d_{5}\right)^{4}+u_{3}\left(d_{2}\right)^{2}\left(d_{5}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+u_{4}d_{2}\left(d_{5}\right)^{3}+u_{5}\left(d_{2}\right)^{3}d_{5}\Big\}.

(379)

We follow the same procedure as with the trigonal case in Sec.
IV.3
.
The diagonalization of the quadratic action, Eq. (
378
),
is accomplished via the orthogonal matrix

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\beta_{+}&-\frac{r_{3}}{\left|r_{3}\right|}\beta_{-}\\
\frac{r_{3}}{\left|r_{3}\right|}\beta_{-}&\beta_{+}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\beta_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{1\pm\frac{\Delta r}{\sqrt{\left(\Delta r\right)^{2}+r_{3}^{2}}}}\,,

with
\Delta r\equiv r_{1}-r_{2}
. We obtain:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{\lambda_{+}\left(d_{+}\right)^{2}+\lambda_{-}\left(d_{-}\right)^{2}\Big\},

(382)

with eigenvalues

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(r_{1}+r_{2}\pm\sqrt{\left(r_{1}-r_{2}\right)^{2}+r_{3}^{2}}\right),

(383)

and eigenvectors

\displaystyle\left(d_{+},d_{-}\right)^{T}

\displaystyle=U^{T}\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}.

(384)

Since
\lambda_{-}<\lambda_{+}
by construction, the combination

d_{-}
orders first. We thus rewrite the action
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}_{-}+\mathcal{S}_{+-}

as

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{-}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{\lambda_{-}\left(d_{-}\right)^{2}+u_{-}\left(d_{-}\right)^{4}\Big\},

(385)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{+-}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{\lambda_{+}\left(d_{+}\right)^{2}+\tilde{u}_{1}d_{+}\left(d_{-}\right)^{3}+\tilde{u}_{2}\left(d_{+}\right)^{2}\left(d_{-}\right)^{2}\Big\},

(386)

where we kept only terms that are linear or quadratic in the sub-leading
channel
d_{+}
and defined

\displaystyle\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_{-}\\
\tilde{u}_{1}\\
\tilde{u}_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\beta_{-}^{4}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_{1}\\
-u_{5}\\
u_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right)+\beta_{+}^{4}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_{2}\\
u_{4}\\
u_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right)+\beta_{-}^{2}\beta_{+}^{2}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_{3}\\
3(u_{5}-u_{4})\\
6u_{1}+6u_{2}-4u_{3}\end{smallmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{r_{3}}{\left|r_{3}\right|}\beta_{-}\beta_{+}^{3}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-u_{4}\\
4u_{2}-2u_{3}\\
3u_{4}-3u_{5}\end{smallmatrix}\right)-\frac{r_{3}}{\left|r_{3}\right|}\beta_{-}^{3}\beta_{+}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}u_{5}\\
4u_{1}-2u_{3}\\
3u_{4}-3u_{5}\end{smallmatrix}\right).

(387)

Minimizing Eq. (
386
) gives
d_{+}\approx-\frac{\tilde{u}_{1}}{2\lambda_{+}}d_{-}^{3}

, which upon reinsertion into
\mathcal{S}_{+-}
leads to a sixth-order
term
d_{-}^{6}
. Hence, fluctuations of the sub-leading channel
only renormalize the sixth-order Landau coefficient of the Ising-nematic
action of the leading channel, Eq. (
385
). To
express the ground state in the in-plane
(\boldsymbol{n}\boldsymbol{m}\boldsymbol{l})
-representation
of Eq. (
114
), we can substitute
d_{+}\approx-\frac{\tilde{u}_{1}}{2\lambda_{+}}d_{-}^{3}

in Eq. (
384
) and perform the inverse transformation
to obtain both
\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{ip}}\right|
and the
specific angle
\gamma_{\mathrm{ip}}
, both of which will be determined
by the Landau parameters of the original action (
378
)-(
379
).

Proceeding to the out-of-plane doublet
\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}
,
the decompositions of the symmetrized products are:

\displaystyle\big[\otimes_{j=1}^{2}\left(E_{g}\oplus\bar{E}_{g}\right)\big]_{s}

\displaystyle=A_{g}\oplus 2B_{g},

(388)

\displaystyle\big[\otimes_{j=1}^{3}\left(E_{g}\oplus\bar{E}_{g}\right)\big]_{s}

\displaystyle=2\left(E_{g}\oplus\bar{E}_{g}\right),

(389)

\displaystyle\big[\otimes_{j=1}^{4}\left(E_{g}\oplus\bar{E}_{g}\right)\big]_{s}

\displaystyle=3A_{g}\oplus 2B_{g},

(390)

with the bilinears
D_{\mathrm{op}}^{A_{g}}=\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|^{2}
,

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{op}}^{B_{g},1}

\displaystyle=\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|^{2}\cos\left(2\gamma_{\mathrm{op}}\right),

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{op}}^{B_{g},2}

\displaystyle=\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|^{2}\sin\left(2\gamma_{\mathrm{op}}\right).

(391)

The four invariants can be expressed as
\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|^{2}
,

\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|^{4}
,
D_{\mathrm{op}}^{B_{g},1}D_{\mathrm{op}}^{B_{g},2}

and
\big(D_{\mathrm{op}}^{B_{g},1}\big)^{2}-\big(D_{\mathrm{op}}^{B_{g},2}\big)^{2}

which, combined with the relationship (
307
),
give the Landau expansion:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathsf{x}}\Big\{r_{0}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|^{2}+u\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|^{4}+v_{4}\left|\boldsymbol{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\right|^{4}\cos\left(4\gamma_{\mathrm{op}}-\delta_{0}\right)\Big\}.

(392)

As in the cases analyzed in the previous Appendices, the offset angle

\delta_{0}
should be understood as a Landau coefficient. The Landau
expansion (
392
) has the shape of a modified
4
-state
clock model,
Z_{4}^{*}
-clock, with the fourfold degenerate angles
offset from the high-symmetry tetragonal directions:

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathrm{op}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\delta_{0}+\frac{2\pi}{4}n+\frac{\pi}{4}\left(\frac{1+\mathrm{sign}v_{4}}{2}\right),

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle\in\{0,1,2,3\}.

(393)

Upon employing Eq. (
114
), we conclude that the
offset angle
\delta_{0}
leads to a rotation of the nematic axes
about the
k_{z}
-axis by an angle
\delta_{0}/4
.
